278: 59:Let F and R be actual reflection/rotation LETTER counts, F+R=4p-1. For F>=1, use the ACTUAL maximal cyclic constant-block representation R_1S_1...R_kS_k, with ell_i>=1, actual rotation multiset X_i in Z_p (0 ALLOWED), r_i=|X_i|>=0, sum ell_i=F,sum r_i=R. 294: 75:    Q<=R-p-1.                                            (Pgap)

507: 5:## 0. Target retained; source/exposure/notation boundary
508: 6:

No pair/nonzero/odd-block/B/kappa/purity/gap/o_F/NF restriction is granted. The theorem below supplies a UNIFORM explicit sufficient criterion on that entire ambient domain, not a claim the criterion always holds. Its complement and concrete failed universal upgrade are stated in full. No word class is silently removed from the assignment.
510: 8:

T0 has only the stated historical single-family boundary in the baseline and is NOT imported as an accepted theorem. Every mechanism used below is freshly proved completely. 512: 10:
513: 11:**Essential notation distinction:** J below is an IDEAL, not a matrix. The algebra is explicitly A=F_p[(F_p,+)^2], isomorphic to F_p[s,t]/(s^p,t^p), and J=(s,t). A parent objection concerned the DIFFERENT matrix J(a)=[[0,a],[1,0]], whose square is aI and which is indeed not nilpotent for a!=0. That matrix, its eigenvalues, and evaluation of a nonzero field element for s or t are NEVER used here. The precise quotient and proof of IDEAL nilpotence are given in Section2. A residue a in a basis symbol E_(a,h) is a GROUP COORDINATE/EXPONENT, not a substitution of a for a nilpotent variable. No matrix nilpotence claim or numerical-check workaround is made.

516: 14:
517: 15:## 1. Ordered group products and elementary selection units
518: 16:
519: 17:Fix an arbitrary prime p>=7. Use
520: 18:
521: 19:    D_(2p)={(x,e):x in F_p,e in{+1,-1}},
522: 20:    (x,e)(x',e')=(x+e x',ee'),
523: 21:    rho_x=(x,+1), sigma_y=(y,-1), identity=(0,+1).
524: 22:
525: 23:For selected letters in increasing order, multiplication by a next(x,e) adds E*x to the first coordinate and multiplies the current second coordinate E by e. This follows by induction directly from the displayed law. In particular any selection with an EVEN number of reflections has product rho_v for a uniquely determined v in F_p.
526: 24:
527: 25:For a selection S with BOTH even cardinality and even reflection count, define
528: 26:
529: 27:    phi(S)=(v(S), |S|/2 modp) in H=(F_p,+)^2.             (1)
530: 28:
531: 29:The integer |S|/2 is taken BEFORE reducing modulo p. If selections S_1,...,S_m lie in consecutive disjoint position blocks, each having even cardinality and even reflection count, then their selected products are rotations and
532: 30:
533: 31:    product(S_1 union ... union S_m)=rho_(sum_i v(S_i)),
534: 32:    phi(S_1 union ... union S_m)=sum_i phi(S_i).          (2)
535: 33:
536: 34:The union is read in block order, which is exactly the ORIGINAL order in that linear word. We do not reorder interleaved subsets or multiply arbitrary noncommuting block products; each complete chosen block product already has second coordinate+1.
537: 35:
538: 36:A *unit* below is an ACTUAL consecutive position block together with a finite list of ACTUAL selectable subsets of that block. Every option has even size and even reflection count, and the EMPTY option is distinguished. Formal coefficients of options are in F_p and need not be nonnegative integers. Coefficients are proof weights, not numbers of occurrences or probabilities.
539: 37:
540: 38:## 2. The EXACT algebra and nilpotence statement
541: 39:
542: 40:Let A=F_p[H]. Concretely, A has F_p-basis E_(a,h), (a,h) in H, with multiplication
543: 41:
544: 42:    E_(a,h) E_(b,k)=E_(a+b,h+k), 1_A=E_(0,0).
545: 43:
546: 44:It is a COMMUTATIVE algebra of dimension p^2 because H is an abelian group of order p^2. Write X=E_(1,0), Y=E_(0,1). Then X^p=Y^p=1_A. Set
547: 45:
548: 46:    s=X-1_A, t=Y-1_A.
549: 47:
550: 48:In characteristic p the binomial coefficients strictly between0 andp vanish. Hence
551: 49:
552: 50:    s^p=(X-1_A)^p=X^p-1_A=0,
553: 51:    t^p=0.                                               (3)
554: 52:
555: 53:The map F_p[s,t]/(s^p,t^p) -> A sending the variables to these elements is surjective: X=1+s,Y=1+t generate every basis element E_(a,h)=X^aY^h, using representatives0..p-1. The quotient has basis s^i t^j for0<=i,j<=p-1 and dimension p^2; therefore this surjective linear map between equal finite dimensions is an isomorphism. In particular
556: 54:
557: 55:    A is isomorphic to F_p[s,t]/(s^p,t^p).                (4)
558: 56:
559: 57:Let epsilon:A->F_p be the augmentation map epsilon(sum c_g E_g)=sum c_g and let J=ker(epsilon). Under(4), epsilon sets s=t=0, so J=(s,t). Every product of 2p-1 elements of J is a sum of monomials of total degree at least2p-1. In any such monomial either the s exponent is at leastp or the t exponent is at leastp; it vanishes by(3). Thus
560: 58:
561: 59:    J^(2p-1)={0}.                                       (5)
562: 60:
563: 61:This is the nilpotence of an IDEAL in the explicit quotient(4), not nilpotence of a nonzero-parameter matrix/operator. The matrix [[0,a],[1,0]] with a!=0 does not satisfy the relations(3) as a proposed image of s; it is not an object in this argument.
564: 62:
565: 63:Modulo J^2, a basis element has expansion
566: 64:
567: 65:    E_(a,h)=(1+s)^a(1+t)^h = 1+a*s+h*t modJ^2.           (6)
568: 66:
569: 67:This is independent of the choice of residue representatives; a,h in the right side are the corresponding elements of F_p. Therefore for theta=sum c_(a,h) E_(a,h),
570: 68:
571: 69:    theta in J^2 iff
572: 70:    sum c_(a,h)=0, sum a*c_(a,h)=0, sum h*c_(a,h)=0.      (7)
573: 71:
574: 72:These are just the constant/s/t coefficients in(4); no external classification or matrix spectral claim is involved.
575: 73:
576: 74:## 3. A depth-one unit for any two letters of the SAME type
577: 75:
578: 76:For an actual two-letter RR block rho_x rho_z, the two allowed options are EMPTY and BOTH letters, with phi values(0,0) and(x+z,1). The formal option sum
579: 77:
580: 78:    theta_RR=1_A-E_(x+z,1) in J                          (8)
581: 79:
582: 80:has coefficient1 on the EMPTY option.
583: 81:
584: 82:For an actual FF block sigma_y sigma_t, BOTH letters multiply to rho_(y-t), so
585: 83:
586: 84:    theta_FF=1_A-E_(y-t,1) in J.                        (9)
587: 85:
588: 86:These statements allow ANY values, including x=0,z=0,y=t and rotations whose sum is0. The coordinate1 in the second component records the actual selection size2. No reflection block parity, pair alphabet or nonzero assumption is introduced.
589: 87:
590: 88:Call either of these an allowed *two-unit*, of depth1.
591: 89:
592: 90:## 4. COMPLETE depth-two criterion for a balanced four-letter block
593: 91:
594: 92:Consider an actual ordered block B with exactly TWO rotations and TWO reflections. Let x,z be its rotation values in their occurrence order, and y,t its reflection values in their occurrence order. Its even-size/even-reflection options are exactly:
595: 93:
596: 94:* EMPTY, phi=(0,0);
597: 95:* its two rotations, phi=(a,1), where a=x+z;
598: 96:* its two reflections, phi=(b,1), where b=y-t;
599: 97:* ALL four letters, phi=(c,2), where c is their ACTUAL ordered-product coordinate.
600: 98:
601: 99:No one-rotation/one-reflection option has even reflection parity. There are no other2letter options of the required parity. All4letters do have even reflection parity. These four actual options are distinct position subsets even when their phi values coincide.
602: 100:
603: 101:We seek a formal option sum with EMPTY coefficient1,
604: 102:
605: 103:    theta_B=1_A+u E_(a,1)+v E_(b,1)+w E_(c,2),           (10)
606: 104:
607: 105:belonging to J^2. By(7), the equations are
608: 106:
609: 107:    1+u+v+w=0,
610: 108:    u+v+2w=0,
611: 109:    a*u+b*v+c*w=0.                                     (11)
612: 110:
613: 111:Subtracting the first equation from the second gives w=1; hence u+v=-2 and a*u+b*v=-c. This is soluble EXACTLY when
614: 112:
615: 113:    a!=b OR c=2a in F_p.                               (GOOD)
616: 114:
617: 115:Indeed:
618: 116:
619: 117:* If a!=b, take
620: 118:
621: 119:       w=1, u=(2b-c)/(a-b), v=(c-2a)/(a-b).             (12)
622: 120:
623: 121:  Their sum u+v=-2 and weighted sum a*u+b*v=-c follow by the displayed numerator identities. Division is only by the stated nonzero field element a-b.
624: 122:* If a=b and c=2a, take w1,u=-2,v0.
625: 123:* If a=b and c!=2a, then u+v=-2 forces a*u+b*v=-2a, which cannot equal-c. Thus NO option sum of form(10) with EMPTY coefficient1 lies in J^2 on these four selections.
626: 124:
627: 125:This proves both sufficiency and the precise FAILURE of this local depth2 ansatz. Failure of(GOOD) is NOT a statement the four-letter block/full word avoids an identity2p selection, and not a no-go for different units/proof methods.
628: 126:
629: 127:For reproducibility, the ACTUAL value c for ALL SIX possible type orders is:
630: 128:
631: 129:| Actual type order and value order | a | b | c |
632: 130:|---|---|---|---|
633: 131:| rho_x rho_z sigma_y sigma_t (RRFF) | x+z | y-t | a+b |
634: 132:| rho_x sigma_y rho_z sigma_t (RFRF) | x+z | y-t | b+x-z |
635: 133:| rho_x sigma_y sigma_t rho_z (RFFR) | x+z | y-t | a+b |
636: 134:| sigma_y rho_x rho_z sigma_t (FRRF) | x+z | y-t | b-a |
637: 135:| sigma_y rho_x sigma_t rho_z (FRFR) | x+z | y-t | b-x+z |
638: 136:| sigma_y sigma_t rho_x rho_z (FFRR) | x+z | y-t | a+b |
639: 137:
640: 138:Every row follows by multiplying left to right with the law in Section1. For example FRRF gives y-x-z-t=b-a; RFRF gives x+y-z-t=b+x-z. We do not confuse a two-rotation selection with the signs of those same rotations in the four-letter selection. The table is a handwritten symbolic case distinction, not enumeration by code.
641: 139:
642: 140:An actual balanced four-letter block satisfying(GOOD) is an allowed *four-unit*, of depth2. All arbitrary/identity-valued rotations and every reflection value remain;(GOOD) is an explicit test, not an ambient target restriction.
643: 141:
644: 142:## 5. The UNIFORM correct-length tiling theorem
645: 143:
646: 144:Define a *TILING certificate* for a word w of length4p-1 as:
647: 145:
648: 146:1. choose an ACTUAL cyclic cut of w, giving a linear cyclic rotation w';
649: 147:2. omit ONE ACTUAL letter occurrence of w', producing its ordered length4p-2 subword u;
650: 148:3. partition ALL positions of u CONSECUTIVELY into allowed two-units and four-units from Sections3–4.
651: 149:
652: 150:The omission/cut/partition are finite explicit combinatorial data. Their existence is NOT assumed for every target word. In particular, each unit has even numbers of both letter types, so the remaining u has even reflection count; the certificate itself enforces that necessary typing condition.
653: 151:
654: 152:> **Theorem TILE (CANDIDATE).** For EVERY prime p>=7 and EVERY word w of length4p-1 over D_(2p), if w has a TILING certificate as just defined, w has EXACTLY2p positions in increasing ORIGINAL index order with identity product.
655: 153:
656: 154:**Proof.** Construct theta_i in A for each actual unit, by(8)/(9)/(10)–(12), with depth d_i=1 or2. Its expansion is explicitly a sum over actual options, each having even size/even reflection parity, with the EMPTY option having coefficient1. Let L_i=2 or4 be its number of positions. Then d_i=L_i/2, and
657: 155:
658: 156:    sum_i d_i=(sum_i L_i)/2=(4p-2)/2=2p-1.
659: 157:
660: 158:Thus Theta=product_i theta_i lies in J^(2p-1)={0} by(5).
661: 159:
662: 160:Expand the product FORMALLY before collecting equal basis points. Choosing one option in each consecutive unit gives an actual selected subset S of u; the union follows unit order and thus u's order. By(2), its basis point is E_(phi(S)). Its coefficient is the product of the option coefficients. The all-EMPTY option choice has actual selected set EMPTY, coefficient1 and basis point E_(0,0). Every other option choice is nonempty, since a nonempty unit option selects2or4 actual positions and units are disjoint.
663: 161:
664: 162:The coefficient of E_(0,0) in Theta=0 is therefore
665: 163:
666: 164:    0 = 1 + sum_(NONEMPTY option choices with phi(S)=0) coefficient(choice).
667: 165:
668: 166:If there were no such nonempty option choice with nonzero coefficient, the right side would be1, a contradiction. Hence at least one NONEMPTY ACTUAL selection S has phi(S)=(0,0). This argument permits coinciding basis points and cancellations; it never mistakes formal option weights for counts. It uses only the unique all-empty choice's coefficient1, not an unproved injectivity/positivity premise.
669: 167:
670: 168:Every selected option has even size and even reflection count, so |S| is a positive even integer at most|u|=4p-2, the selected product is rho_(v(S)), and phi(S)=0 gives v(S)=0 and |S|/2=0 modp. Consequently
671: 169:
672: 170:    1<=|S|/2<=2p-1 and p divides |S|/2,
673: 171:
674: 172:so |S|/2=p and |S|=2p. Its product is rho_0=identity.
675: 173:
676: 174:The selected positions of u embed increasingly into w' by skipping the one omitted occurrence; their order/product is unchanged. Finally write the original word as AB so that w'=BA. Let P_A,P_B be the selected ordered products in the respective original portions. The constructed identity in w' says P_B P_A=1. The ORIGINAL increasing-index product is
677: 175:
678: 176:    P_A P_B=P_A(P_B P_A)P_A^(-1)=1.
679: 177:
680: 178:The same2p ORIGINAL positions therefore give identity in the ORIGINAL index order. This remains valid when one portion's selection is empty, when a unit crosses the omitted position in w' (that position is simply not selected), and for cuts through any letter type. No rearrangement of selected noncommuting letters, inverse normalization or old4p-2theorem transfer occurs. QED.
681: 179:
682: 180:This theorem applies uniformly at the full correct-length ambient word domain; the added certificate is an explicit sufficient hypothesis, not a concealed reduction of the stated whole target. The unresolved complement is Section8.
683: 181:
684: 182:## 6. A concrete UNBOUNDED certificate class beyond the basic pair/o_F/numerical bands
685: 183:
686: 184:For EVERY p>=7 and ANY2p choices x_0,...,x_(2p-1) in{1,2}, form the actual word
687: 185:
688: 186:    W(x)=rho_(x_0) sigma_0 rho_(x_1) sigma_0 ...
689: 187:         rho_(x_(2p-2)) sigma_0 rho_(x_(2p-1)).            (13)
690: 188:
691: 189:It has R=2p,F=2p-1,N4p-1 and o_F=2p-1 singleton cyclic constant reflection blocks. The first and last rotations are consecutive across the cyclic boundary, so every reflection is still cyclically singleton. Delete the FIRST sigma_0 in this displayed linear word. Its first two remaining rotations form one RR two-unit. The rest partitions, consecutively, into p-1 units of type
692: 190:
693: 191:    sigma_0 rho_(x_(2j)) sigma_0 rho_(x_(2j+1)),
694: 192:    1<=j<=p-1.
695: 193:
696: 194:For each such FRFR unit, b=0, a is one of2,3,4, and c=-x_(2j)+x_(2j+1). Since p>=7, a!=0=b, so(GOOD) holds, with no need to constrain c. TheoremTILE supplies an original-order identity2p selection for EVERY assignment in(13), not a list of sampled parameters.
697: 195:
698: 196:This includes genuinely NON-OPPOSITE-PAIR rotation alphabets: if both1and2 occur, {1,2} is not contained in{c,-c} for p>=7. Thus this class is not an invocation of RGR-LONG's pair hypothesis. It has o_F=2p-1>p, outside the old ASSEMBLY-P numerical odd-block class. Its countR2p is below3p-1; its cyclic gaps consist of one2rotation gap and the other1rotation gaps, so Q1 and F+Q2p<3p-1, outside the universal P criterion.
699: 197:
700: 198:For a sharper baseline comparison, choose exactly p-1 values1 and p+1 values2 in ANY order. The only rotation-only2p selection is the full rotation multiset, whose sum is(p-1)+2(p+1)=3p+1=1 modp, so that exact old rotation-only test fails. Every gap is nonempty; for every Davenport-alpha I, |I|+rotationsoutsideI<=R=2p<3p-1, so that filter also fails. Both rotation multiplicities are at leastp-1; making the alphabet an opposite pair by deleting letters needs at leastp-1 deletions, beyond the historical Rprime p-3 allowance. Ordinary reflection runs are2p-1 singleton odd runs, so historical T0's at-most-one-odd-run hypothesis fails.
701: 199:
702: 200:**Credit qualification:** this does NOT establish disjointness from EVERY old raw Doubling or qualified4p-2deletion-provider instance, and NO all-old-provider-new-cell count/novelty is claimed. Any such overlap keeps OLD credit. The new manuscript proves a uniform explicit local-block/correct-length sufficient criterion and a genuine unrestricted-value word class, not a full literature/SOTA improvement or a proof that all these words were previously unresolved. This exact distinction prevents merely relabeling old coverage as discovery.
703: 201:
704: 202:## 7. A COMPLETE uniform counterexample to UNIVERSALITY OF THIS CERTIFICATE (NOT to Olson)
705: 203:
706: 204:There is a concrete first failed overreach. A balanced block is not automatically good: for
707: 205:
708: 206:    B=rho_1 sigma_0 rho_-1 sigma_0,
709: 207:
710: 208:one has a=b=0,c=2!=0 in F_p. By Section4, no theta of form(10) with EMPTY coefficient1 lies in J^2. This is a LOCAL method failure only.
711: 209:
712: 210:More strongly, for every prime p>=7 the correct-length word
713: 211:
714: 212:    V_p=(rho_1 sigma_0 rho_-1 sigma_0)^(p-1)
715: 213:         rho_1 sigma_0 rho_-1                              (14)
716: 214:
717: 215:has NO TILING certificate, even though it DOES have an identity2p subword. This shows the certificate class is a proper partial class of the ambient target, not an unproved universal hypothesis disguised as a method.
718: 216:
719: 217:**Actual domain and solvability.** There are2p rotations in(14), alternating values+1,-1 in their cyclic occurrence order, and2p-1 singleton sigma_0 reflections. The word has length4p-1. Selecting ALL2p rotations gives value p-p=0 and no reflection, so identity2p already exists by the old rotation-only test. Thus(14) is emphatically NOT a counterexample to the target.
720: 218:
721: 219:**No-certificate proof.** Every allowed unit contains an even number of reflections. OriginalF=2p-1 is odd, so any possible one-letter omission in a certificate MUST remove a reflection, not a rotation. After omitting a reflection, all remaining reflections are still singleton and every two adjacent rotation OCCURRENCES in rotation-occurrence order have opposite values. No remaining FF adjacency exists.
722: 220:
723: 221:Cyclically the original word has exactly ONE RR adjacency edge: the last/first rotations. Removing one reflection adds exactly ONE more RR adjacency edge between its former neighboring rotations, so after deletion the cyclic remaining word has exactly TWO RR adjacency edges. If the removed reflection adjoins the old2rotation gap, that gap becomes3rotations and has two edges; otherwise there are two2rotation gaps with one edge each. This is a direct adjacency count, independent of where a subsequent linear cut is placed. A linear cut can remove a cyclic edge from the linear adjacency list, never add one.
724: 222:
725: 223:Classify a possible unit in this remaining word:
726: 224:
727: 225:* A two-unit cannot be FF (no FF adjacency). Therefore it must be RR, and it uses an RR adjacency edge.
728: 226:* A balanced four-unit cannot be RRFF,RFFR,orFFRR, because these require an FF adjacency. If it is RFRF or FRFR, its two rotations are adjacent in rotation-occurrence order and opposite, so a0,b0 while c is respectively +2or-2, nonzero; the unit is BAD by(GOOD). The only possible GOOD balanced four-unit type is FRRF, which contains an RR adjacency edge (and in fact has a=b=c0, so is good).
729: 227:
730: 228:Thus EVERY allowed unit in any possible certificate must contain exactly TWO rotation occurrences and use an RR adjacency edge. Distinct disjoint consecutive units cannot use the same RR edge because that edge's endpoint positions would have to belong to both units. At most TWO allowed units can occur, as only two RR edges exist. But all2p remaining rotation occurrences must be covered and each allowed unit contains exactly2, requiring p units. Since p>=7>2, this is impossible.
731: 229:
732: 230:This exhausts EVERY cyclic cut, EVERY possible omitted letter and EVERY allowed2/4unit partition, without enumeration or a program. Therefore(14) lacks the certificate. 4: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-theoremP.md
5: SHA256 58a85ebeba8208e2f89b39a691235d2d1374656d8888ea905fe96aa75e1dafc2
6: LF 1-109
7: 1:# Theorem P: a Chevalley–Warning proof of ordered EGZ for a class of dihedral words
8: 2:
9: 3:Status: written proof (lane MathOlson, 2026-10-16), mechanism checked by brute
10: 4:force (`code/verify_marked.py`, `code/verify_theoremP.py`); NOT yet independently
11: 5:reviewed.  This is a theorem about a CLASS OF WORDS in D_{2p}; it does not
12: 6:prove ordered Olson for D_{2p} (the "many odd gaps" class remains open here).
13: 7:
14: 8:## Setting and conventions
15: 9:
16: 10:p is an odd prime. D_{2p} = {(v, e) : v in Z_p, e in {+1,-1}} with
17: 11:(v, e)(v', e') = (v + e v', e e')  (rotations (x,+1), reflections (y,-1)).
18: 12:For a word w = w_1 ... w_N and indices i_1 < ... < i_k the ordered product is
19: 13:w_{i_1} w_{i_2} ... w_{i_k}.  An identity k-subword is such an index set with
20: 14:product (0, +1).  Cyclic rotation of w preserves the existence of identity
21: 15:k-subwords (a product U V becomes V U = U^{-1} (U V) U).
22: 16:
23: 17:Let w contain F >= 1 reflections.  Rotate w cyclically so that it ENDS with a
24: 18:reflection:
25: 19:    w = X_1 f_1 X_2 f_2 ... X_F f_F,
26: 20:where f_t = (y_t, -1) are the reflections and X_t (t = 1..F) is the block of
27: 21:rotations strictly between f_{t-1} and f_t (X_1 = rotations before f_1); n_t =
28: 22:|X_t|.  ("Gap t" = X_t; cyclically these are exactly the gaps between
29: 23:consecutive reflections.)
30: 24:
31: 25:## Theorem P
32: 26:
33: 27:If N = |w| <= 4p - 1 and  F + sum_{t=1}^{F} floor(n_t / 2) >= 3p - 1,  then w
34: 28:has an identity 2p-subword.
35: 29:
36: 30:## Proof
37: 31:
38: 32:**Step 1 (parametrisation).** In each gap X_t list the rotations in index order
39: 33:and group them into floor(n_t/2) consecutive PAIRS pi = (i, i'); if n_t is odd
40: 34:the last rotation of X_t is a SINGLETON.  Introduce Boolean parameters
41: 35:  u_2, ..., u_F  (gap colours; set u_1 := 0 and u_{F+1} := u_1 = 0),
42: 36:  m_pi  for each pair pi  (pair marks).
43: 37:Number of parameters:  M = (F - 1) + sum_t floor(n_t/2) >= 3p - 2.
44: 38:
45: 39:To a parameter point (u, m) assign the selection S(u, m):
46: 40:* reflection f_t is selected iff u_t != u_{t+1}  (t = 1..F);
47: 41:* both rotations of pair pi in gap t are selected iff m_pi XOR u_t = 1,
48: 42:  otherwise neither;
49: 43:* singletons are never selected.
50: 44:
51: 45:**Step 2 (injectivity; the empty selection).** The selection determines the
52: 46:parameters: going left to right, u_1 = 0 and u_{t+1} = u_t XOR [f_t selected]
53: 47:determine all u_t; then m_pi = [pi selected] XOR u_t.  Hence the map
54: 48:(u, m) -> S(u, m) is injective, and S(u, m) is EMPTY iff (u, m) = 0: an empty
55: 49:selection selects no reflection, so all u_t = u_1 = 0, and no pair, so all
56: 50:m_pi = u_t = 0.
57: 51:
58: 52:**Step 3 (size).** |S(u,m)| = #{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} + 2 #{pi : m_pi XOR u_{t(pi)} = 1}.
59: 53:The first term is the number of colour changes around the cycle 1..F, 1, hence
60: 54:even.  So |S| is always EVEN, and |S| <= N <= 4p - 1.
61: 55:
62: 56:**Step 4 (signs and the product).** Let S = S(u,m).  For an element of S lying
63: 57:in gap t or equal to f_t, the number of selected reflections strictly before
64: 58:it is #{s < t : u_s != u_{s+1}}, which has the parity of [u_t != u_1] = u_t.
65: 59:So in the ordered product every selected rotation x in gap t is multiplied by
66: 60:c_t := (-1)^{u_t}, and a selected reflection f_t contributes c_t y_t to the
67: 61:rotation coordinate.  The number of selected reflections is even (Step 3), so
68: 62:the product is (V, +1) with
69: 63:  V = sum_{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} c_t y_t + sum_{pairs pi selected} c_{t(pi)} x_pi,
70: 64:where x_pi = x_i + x_i'.  Using (c_t - c_{t+1})/2 = u_{t+1} - u_t (which is
71: 65:0 when u_t = u_{t+1} and c_t when they differ) and summation by parts with
72: 66:u_1 = u_{F+1} = 0,
73: 67:  sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_{t+1} - u_t) y_t = sum_{t=2}^{F} u_t (y_{t-1} - y_t).
74: 68:For pairs: a selected pair (m XOR u_t = 1) contributes c_t x_pi; one checks
75: 69:c_t x_pi [m XOR u_t] = x_pi m - x_pi u_t for both u_t = 0, 1.  Therefore, in Z_p,
76: 70:  V(u, m) = sum_{t=2}^{F} beta_t u_t + sum_pi x_pi m_pi,
77: 71:  beta_t = y_{t-1} - y_t - sum_{pi in gap t} x_pi.
78: 72:V is a polynomial of degree 1 in the parameters (over F_p), and S(u,m) is an
79: 73:identity subword iff V(u, m) = 0 in Z_p.
80: 74:
81: 75:**Step 5 (Chevalley–Warning on the Boolean box).** Write the size as the
82: 76:integer polynomial
83: 77:  Z(u, m) = sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_t + u_{t+1} - 2 u_t u_{t+1}) + 2 sum_pi (m_pi + u_{t(pi)} - 2 m_pi u_{t(pi)}),
84: 78:which agrees with |S(u, m)| on {0,1}^M and has degree 2.  Over F_p put
85: 79:  Q = (1 - V^{p-1}) (1 - Z^{p-1}),   deg Q <= 3(p - 1) < M.
86: 80:On {0,1}^M, Q(x) = 1 if V(x) = 0 and Z(x) = 0 in F_p, and Q(x) = 0 otherwise
87: 81:(Fermat).  Box lemma: for any polynomial Q over F_p of degree < M,
88: 82:  sum_{x in {0,1}^M} (-1)^{|x|} Q(x) = 0.
89: 83:(Proof: suffices for monomials; a monomial of degree < M omits some variable
90: 84:x_j, and the involution flipping coordinate j pairs points with opposite signs
91: 85:and equal values.)  Hence
92: 86:  sum_{x : V(x) = 0, Z(x) = 0 mod p} (-1)^{|x|} = 0 in F_p.
93: 87:The point x = 0 contributes +1, so there is a parameter point x != 0 with
94: 88:V(x) = 0 and |S(x)| = Z(x) = 0 mod p.
95: 89:
96: 90:**Step 6 (conclusion).** By Step 2, S(x) is nonempty; by Step 3 its size is
97: 91:even and at most 4p - 1; being divisible by p (odd), the size is a positive
98: 92:even multiple of p below 4p, i.e. exactly 2p.  By Step 4 its ordered product is
99: 93:the identity.  QED.
100: 94:
101: 95:## Remarks
102: 96:
103: 97:1. Corollary (reflection coset): any word of 3p-1 reflections in D_{2p} has an
104: 98:   identity 2p-subword (F = 3p-1, all n_t = 0).  Finite-exact computations
105: 99:   (m = 3..9) show 3m-1 is also the sharp threshold for composite m.
106: 100:2. Which words are covered depends only on the R/F type pattern.  Uncovered:
107: 101:   F - #(odd gaps) <= 2p - 2; this contains every extremal (length 4p-2)
108: 102:   dihedral avoider found in the exhaustive D10 data (alternating odd blocks).
109: 103:3. The 2-part (size parity) is handled ONLY through pairing inside gaps.  A
110: 104:   general "mixed modulus" Chevalley–Warning statement that would also control
111: 105:   parity is false (it would give D(G) = D*(G) for G = C_2^4 + C_6).
112: 106:4. Prime powers m = p^k: the same argument with congruences mod p^k would need
113: 107:   Brink's restricted-variable Chevalley theorem for prime-power moduli
114: 108:   (Combinatorica 31 (2011) 127-130); its exact hypotheses have NOT been read
115: 109:   from the source here, so the extension is CONDITIONAL and kept separate.

151: 4:
152: 5:1. **Original order:** call the original word AB and the rotated word w′=BA. Cutting after a reflection only cyclically permutes the gap lengths, preserving F,N and sum floor(n_t/2). If P_A,P_B are the selected ordered products in the two parts, the rotated product is P_B P_A and the original product is P_A P_B=P_B^−1(P_B P_A)P_B. Thus the same selected2p positions give identity in the original increasing-index order, including pairs crossing the original boundary.
153: 6:2. **Product formula:** induction from (A,E)(a,e)=(A+Ea,Ee) gives (a_1,e_1)…(a_k,e_k)=(sum_j (product_(l<j)e_l)a_j, product_j e_j). Prefix reflection parity therefore supplies the sign c_t stated in Step4.
154: 7:3. **Notation/degrees:** use degV≤1, degZ≤2 (only upper bounds are needed); write wt(x) for the parameter-vector Hamming weight, distinct from selected length |S(x)|. Write |S(x)|=Z(x)≡0(modp) rather than an equality-to-congruence chain. Codex's exact-degree1 finding is already disposed byv1; weakening the degree2 wording changes no step.

158: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/rotation/notes-GRX-v1.md
159: SHA256 afc6c31a2f9c9502655e3666595e535f4a003ccd7670ddbe993ebe4b620b1a30
160: LF 1-54
161: 1:# Lemma GRX: exact solvability criterion for EVERY word over D_(2p) (OB1)
162: 2:
163: 3:Owner OlsonRotationRigidity.  Written after host clock 2026-10-18T01:52:10-07:00.  Status [W] unreviewed; the
164: 4:statement is a direct generalisation of Lemma GR (olson/proof-GR.md Sec. 2, reviewed) and Lemma GR0
165: 5:(c2/invstar/proof-GR0-r1.md, unreviewed); the proof below is self-contained.  Controls [F] in Sec. 3.
166: 6:
167: 7:## 1. Representation and statement
168: 8:
169: 9:p odd prime, conventions of olson/proof-dihedral-partial.md Sec. 0.  Let w have F >= 1 reflections.  Read w
170: 10:cyclically and cut every maximal cyclic reflection run at each change of value: w is a cyclic alternation
171: 11:    R_1 S_1 R_2 S_2 ... R_k S_k,   S_i = sigma_(y_i)^(l_i) (l_i >= 1),   R_i = rotations with value multiset X_i,
172: 12:|X_i| = r_i >= 0 (R_i may be EMPTY; then y_(i-1) != y_i when the blocks are maximal, but the criterion below holds
173: 13:for every such representation, maximal or not).  y_0 := y_k, theta_i := y_(i-1) - y_i, sum theta_i = 0.
174: 14:For z in {+-1}^k: f_i = [z_i != z_(i+1)] (z_(k+1) = z_1), Phi = sum f_i (even), delta = #{i : l_i != f_i mod 2},
175: 15:C_max = F - delta, X^z := multiset union over i of z_i X_i (each value multiplied by z_i), R = sum r_i,
176: 16:T(z) := sum_{i : z_i = +1} theta_i (mod p), and Sigma_m(Y) := set of sums of size-m sub-multisets of Y.
177: 17:
178: 18:**Lemma GRX.**  w has an identity 2p-subword iff there are z and an even C with Phi <= C <= C_max, 0 <= 2p - C <= R
179: 19:and T(z) in Sigma_(2p-C)(X^z).
180: 20:(F = 0: identity 2p-subword iff 0 in Sigma_(2p)(X); rotations commute.  The sign pattern may be normalised z_1 = +1.)
181: 21:
182: 22:## 2. Proof
183: 23:
184: 24:A selection with an even number of reflections is given by (i) z_i := (-1)^(number of selected reflections
185: 25:cyclically before R_i, counted from a fixed cut point at the end of S_k), (ii) c_i := number of selected letters of
186: 26:S_i, with c_i = f_i (mod 2) [the parity of selected reflections in S_i is exactly what changes the zone sign between
187: 27:R_i and R_(i+1)], (iii) any sub-multiset of each X_i.  Conversely each such datum with 0 <= c_i <= l_i is realised
188: 28:(choose any c_i letters of the constant block S_i; any chosen rotations).  Because S_i is constant, its c_i selected
189: 29:letters multiply to sigma_(y_i) (c_i odd) or 1 (c_i even); so the ordered product, read from the cut, is
190: 30:    rho_V,  V = sum_i z_i (sum of chosen values in X_i) + sum_{i : f_i = 1} z_i y_i      (mod p),
191: 31:by induction along the word (a rotation rho_x in a zone of sign z contributes z x; a reflection sigma_y entered in
192: 32:a zone of sign z contributes z y and flips the zone sign; the even total makes the product a rotation).
193: 33:Summation by parts: sum_{f_i=1} z_i y_i = sum_i y_i (z_i - z_(i+1))/2 = sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1))/2 = -(sum_i z_i theta_i)/2,
194: 34:and sum_i z_i theta_i = 2 sum_{z_i=+1} theta_i - sum_i theta_i = 2 T(z).  Hence V = sigma - T(z), sigma := the sum of
195: 35:the chosen elements of X^z.  The size is C + m with C = sum c_i, m = number of chosen rotations.  For fixed z the
196: 36:realisable C are exactly the even numbers in [Phi, C_max]: c_i ranges over the integers in [0, l_i] congruent to
197: 37:f_i mod 2, i.e. from f_i up to l_i - [l_i != f_i mod 2]; summing, C ranges over all values of the right parity
198: 38:between Phi and F - delta (each c_i moves in steps of 2), and Phi + C_max = F - delta + Phi is even because F = sum l_i
199: 39:= delta + Phi (mod 2).  The rotations are chosen independently of the c_i.  So the selection is an identity
200: 40:2p-subword iff C + m = 2p and sigma = T(z), as claimed.  The global sign change z -> -z maps X^z -> -X^z and
201: 41:T(z) -> sum_{z_i=-1} theta_i = -T(z), so z_1 = +1 is a normalisation.  Identity subwords are invariant under cyclic
202: 42:rotation of w (UV = 1 iff VU = 1), so the cyclic reading is exact.  []
203: 43:
204: 44:Specialisations: all rotation values in {1, -1} gives Lemma GR (Sigma_m(X^z) is then the window
205: 45:{m - 2q : max(0, m - E+) <= q <= min(m, E-)}, E+- as in GR), and with empty runs Lemma GR0.
206: 46:
207: 47:## 3. Controls [F] (code/grx.py, code/rawdp.py, code/test_grx_v1.py; output tmp/test_grx_v1.out.json)
208: 48:
209: 49:GRX (literal implementation) == fresh raw DP == unchanged olson/code/was_verify.py dp_has on every word:

246: 28:
247: 29:- **M1 required:** PM*, K1 and K2 are stated for any cyclic constant-block representation, allowing empty rotation gaps and adjacent equal blocks. Maximality is required only when defining the original invariant o for ROT-o2. The proofs themselves use no maximality, so their iteration is valid.
248: 30:- **M2 required:** explicitly name the group law above and the even selected-reflection parity. Under the opposite convention the sign of T changes; do not mix conventions. For a literal rho_V product formula first normalize z1=+1; global sign reversal preserves the zero condition.
249: 31:- **M3:** cancellation lengths1+e_h are even. The cumulative threshold crossing in K2 uses that parity to bound the pre-crossing total by2t and the crossing by4t.
250: 32:- **M4:** if o=0, PM* leaves one even block but K1 forces the reflection count2p-1 odd, contradiction. Do not leave the exclusion implicit.
251: 33:- **M5:** use a new symbol for cumulative cancellation lengths, distinguish sequence terms from partial sums, and define H=sum floor(l_i/2). “Only pattern” in K1 means up to global sign; the symbol for a signed sum is not a reflection element.
252: 34:- **M6:** the final parity relation is always o≡R mod2 at total length4p-2; replace the garbled parenthetical accordingly.
253: 35:- **M7:** reviewer observes the argument may extend to p=3. This verdict keeps p>=5 and does not promote that extension.

264: SHA256 59fde8a64a336eef9526bf6d9c71c9060c3f24ab7c9d2ffd6acf8c950dcad5d1
265: LF 1-139
266: 1:# Ordered EGZ in D_{2p}: proved components, dependency map, exact residual
267: 2:
268: 3:Lane MathOlson, 2026-10-16 (PDT).  Evidence labels: **[W]** written proof;

274: 9:## 0. Conventions
275: 10:
276: 11:p odd prime; D_{2p} = {(v,e)}, (v,e)(v',e') = (v + e v', e e'); rotations
277: 12:rho_x = (x,+1), reflections sigma_y = (y,-1).  w is a word of length N; an
278: 13:identity 2p-subword is an increasing index set of size 2p with ordered product
279: 14:(0,+1).  Existence is invariant under cyclic rotation of w and under
280: 15:automorphisms (rho_v -> rho_{uv}, sigma_v -> sigma_{uv+c}, u != 0).
281: 16:
282: 17:Gap model (F >= 1 reflections): cyclically w = X_1 f_1 X_2 f_2 ... X_F f_F,
283: 18:f_t = sigma_{y_t}; X_t = block of n_t rotations before f_t ("gap t"); R = sum n_t.
284: 19:Bracket product of gap t: beta_t = y_{t-1} - y_t - (sum of X_t).
285: 20:Marked formulation (proof-theoremP.md, Steps 1-4): a colouring u of gaps
286: 21:(u_t = 1 "Minus") with one gap fixed to 0 and marks m_r for rotations encode
287: 22:every selection with an even number of reflections bijectively (reflection f_t
288: 23:selected iff u_t != u_{t+1}; rotation r selected iff m_r XOR u_gap(r) = 1); the
289: 24:ordered product is rho_V with V = sum_{t in Minus} beta_t + sum_{marked} x_r,
290: 25:and the size is Z = #{colour changes} + #{selected rotations}.
291: 26:
292: 27:## 1. Lemma EGZ [W, classical]
293: 28:If R >= 3p-1, an identity 2p-subword exists (2p rotations with zero sum).
294: 29:
295: 30:## 2. Theorem P [W][R][C]  (proof-theoremP.md; review/codex-theoremP/receipt.json)
296: 31:If N <= 4p-1 and F + sum_t floor(n_t/2) >= 3p-1, an identity 2p-subword exists.
297: 32:
298: 33:## 3. Doubling Lemma [W][C]
299: 34:Let N = 4p-1, F >= 1.  Let 0 <= j and a cyclic stretch of 2j+1 distinct
300: 35:consecutive gaps t_0, ..., t_0+2j be given; call t_0+2i (i = 0..j) P-gaps and
301: 36:t_0+2i-1 (i = 1..j) M-gaps.  Suppose a set K of rotations lying in P-gaps has
302: 37:|K| = p - 2j - sum_{M-gaps} n_t >= 0 and
303: 38:    sum_{M-gaps} beta_t + sum_{r in K} x_r = 0 (mod p).
304: 39:Then w has an identity 2p-subword.
305: 40:
306: 41:Proof.  U := {f_{t-1}, f_t : t an M-gap} u {all rotations in M-gaps} u K.
307: 42:|U| = 2j + sum n_M + |K| = p, and U is the selection with colouring "M-gaps
308: 43:Minus, all other gaps Plus" and marks = K, so its product is rho_V with
309: 44:V = sum_M beta_t + sum_K x = 0: U is an identity p-subword.
310: 45:V-family: freeze all 2j+1 stretch gaps to Plus (one of them is the fixed gap);
311: 46:free parameters are the colours of the F-2j-1 other gaps and one mark for each
312: 47:rotation not in U (R - (p - 2j) marks; rotations of M-gaps all lie in U).
313: 48:M = F + R - p - 1 = 3p - 2 parameters.  The V-selection never contains U's
314: 49:letters (U's reflections lie between frozen gaps; U's rotations are not
315: 50:parameters).  The marked value is linear and the size quadratic, so the box
316: 51:Chevalley-Warning argument of Theorem P (deg Q <= 3(p-1) < M) gives a nonzero
317: 52:parameter point with V-value 0 and V-size = 0 mod p; injectivity gives
318: 53:V-size >= 1, and V avoids U so V-size <= 4p-1-p = 3p-1; hence V-size in {p, 2p}.
319: 54:If 2p, done.  If p, the union U u V is the selection with Minus(U u V) =
320: 55:Minus(V) u M-gaps (the M-gaps are isolated between frozen Plus gaps) and
321: 56:marked(U u V) = marked(V) u K; by the marked formula its value is
322: 57:value(V) + 0 = 0 and its size is 2p.  QED.
323: 58:Special case j = 0: some gap contains p rotations with zero sum (e.g. p equal
324: 59:rotations, or any gap with >= 2p-1 rotations by EGZ).
325: 60:
326: 61:## 4. Lemma Davenport-alpha [W]
327: 62:Let F >= 2 and let I be a set of pairwise non-adjacent gaps (cyclically).  Items:
328: 63:(2, y_{t-1} - y_t) for t in I (empty bracket: both reflections of gap t,
329: 64:rotations of gap t unselected) and (1, x_r) for every rotation r outside the
330: 65:gaps of I.  Any subset of items is a valid selection (Minus = chosen bracket
331: 66:gaps, all isolated) whose (size, value) is the item sum.  Items live in
332: 67:Z_{2p} + Z_p, whose Davenport constant is 3p - 1 (rank-2 formula
333: 68:D(Z_m + Z_n) = m + n - 1 for m | n; Olson 1969 / van Emde Boas-Kruyswijk).
334: 69:If |I| + #(rotations outside I-gaps) >= 3p - 1, a nonempty item subset sums to
335: 70:(0,0); its size is a positive multiple of 2p below 4p, i.e. 2p.  QED.
336: 71:(F = 1 excluded: the single gap's "bracket" would use the sole reflection twice;
337: 72:review fix, Codex review/codex-T0-R.  For F = 1, R = 4p-2 >= 3p-1 and Lemma EGZ
338: 73:applies anyway.)
339: 74:
340: 75:## 5. Theorem R (rigid words) [W][C]
341: 76:If N = 4p-1, p >= 3, and every letter of w lies in {rho_x, rho_{-x}, sigma_y}
342: 77:for some x != 0 and y, then w has an identity 2p-subword.
343: 78:
344: 79:Proof.  Normalise x = 1, y = 0 by an automorphism.  If F >= 2p take 2p
345: 80:reflections (sigma_0^{2p} = 1).  If F = 0 use Lemma EGZ.  If some maximal run
346: 81:of rotations (a gap) contains >= p copies of rho_1 or of rho_{-1}, use the
347: 82:Doubling Lemma with j = 0.  Otherwise 1 <= F <= 2p-1 and every rotation run
348: 83:R_i (i = 1..k, alternating cyclically with reflection runs S_i of lengths
349: 84:l_i >= 1) has net d_i = #rho_1 - #rho_{-1} with |d_i| <= p-1.
350: 85:Selections: for a sign pattern z in {+-1}^k let f_i = [z_i != z_{i+1}]
351: 86:(cyclic, so sum f_i is even); pick c_i reflections from S_i with c_i = f_i
352: 87:(mod 2), 0 <= c_i <= l_i (the order is irrelevant since all are sigma_0), and
353: 88:any rotations.  Rotations of R_i get sign z_i up to one common global sign (the
354: 89:realised sign of the first selected zone is +; a global sign change preserves
355: 90:value 0, |D|, delta and min(E+,E-); review fix, Codex review/codex-T0-R);
356: 91:reflections contribute 0.
357: 92:With E+ = sum_{z_i=+} a_i + sum_{z_i=-} b_i (a_i, b_i = numbers of rho_1,
358: 93:rho_{-1} in R_i), E- = R - E+, D(z) = E+ - E- = sum z_i d_i, and
359: 94:delta(z) = #{i : f_i != l_i mod 2}, the reflection count C = sum c_i can be any
360: 95:even number in [sum f_i, F - delta(z)].  Choosing A effectively-positive and A
361: 96:effectively-negative rotations gives value 0; size 2A + C = 2p is achievable
362: 97:with A <= min(E+, E-) iff |D(z)| + delta(z) <= R + F - 2p = 2p - 1 (note
363: 98:sum f_i <= k <= F <= 2p-1).
364: 99:Choice of z: natural flips f_i = l_i mod 2 for i < k, f_k fixed by parity, so
365: 100:delta <= 1.  Let d*_i = z*_i d_i, P_j = d*_1 + ... + d*_j.  The walk P_0 = 0,
366: 101:..., P_k = D* has steps of size <= p-1, so some P_j' is within (p-1)/2 of D*/2.
367: 102:If j' in {0,k} then |D*| <= p-1 and z* works.  Otherwise flip the signs of R_1..
368: 103:R_j' (toggle f_k and f_j'): |D| = |D* - 2P_j'| <= p-1 and delta <= 3, so
369: 104:|D| + delta <= p+2 <= 2p-1 for p >= 3.  QED.
370: 105:[C] code/rigid_construct.py builds the explicit selection and multiplies it out;
371: 106:no failure in 20000 random rigid words for each p in {3,5,7,11,13}.
372: 107:[C] The rigid feasibility reduction (|D|+delta <= 2p-1) agreed with exact DP on
373: 108:6600 random rigid words (p = 3,5,7).
374: 109:
375: 110:### 5.1 Corollary R' (rigid with few removable defects) [W]
376: 111:If deleting a set Delta of at most p-3 letters leaves a rigid word and every
377: 112:maximal rotation run of w - Delta that contains >= p equal template letters lies
378: 113:inside a single gap of w, then w has an identity 2p-subword: apply the proof of
379: 114:Theorem R to w - Delta (length 4p-1-|Delta|, bound 2p-1-|Delta| >= p+2), with the
380: 115:Doubling Lemma applied to w itself for runs with >= p equal template rotations;
381: 116:all other runs of w - Delta have net at most p-1.

406: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-T0.md
407: SHA256 40a515da31f02e0e0c6627f1a78b76730ff81b4d7ea0905177724ecffd3dbd25
408: LF 1-125
409: 1:# Theorem T0 (group-algebra tiling with rotation nesting) for D_{2p}
410: 2:
411: 3:Lane MathOlson, written 2026-10-16 ~21:15 PDT (host clock 21:11:52 read just before
412: 4:writing).  Status: written proof [W]; computer sanity checks [C] listed at the end;
413: 5:NOT yet independently reviewed.
414: 6:
415: 7:## Statement
416: 8:
417: 9:**Theorem T0.** Let p be an odd prime and w a word of length 4p-1 over D_{2p}.
418: 10:Read cyclically, w is a cyclic sequence of maximal runs of reflections
419: 11:("F-runs") separated by maximal runs of rotations.  If at most one F-run has odd
420: 12:length, then w has an identity 2p-subword.
421: 13:
422: 14:(No condition on the values of the letters.)
423: 15:
424: 16:## Setting
425: 17:
426: 18:Conventions as in proof-theoremP.md: (v,e)(v',e') = (v+ev', ee'), rho_x = (x,+1),
427: 19:sigma_y = (y,-1).  For a finite sequence of letters, its ordered product is
428: 20:(sum_i s_i v_i, prod e_i) with s_i = (-1)^{#reflections before i}.  Existence of
429: 21:an identity 2p-subword is invariant under cyclic rotation of w (ab = 1 iff ba = 1),
430: 22:so we may cut the cyclic word anywhere and work with the resulting linear word.
431: 23:
432: 24:H := Z_p x Z_p, A := F_p[H], J := augmentation ideal.  With s := [(1,0)]-1 and
433: 25:t := [(0,1)]-1, A = F_p[s,t]/(s^p, t^p) and J = (s,t).
434: 26:
435: 27:**Fact L (Loewy length).** J^{2p-1} = 0.  Proof: J^{2p-1} is spanned by products
436: 28:containing monomials s^i t^j with i+j >= 2p-1, hence i >= p or j >= p.  []
437: 29:
438: 30:For a selection T (set of positions) with an even number of reflections, the
439: 31:ordered product is a rotation rho_{val(T)}; if moreover |T| is even put
440: 32:phi(T) := (val(T), |T|/2) in H.
441: 33:
442: 34:**Fact A (additivity).**
443: 35:(a) If T_1, ..., T_m lie in consecutive disjoint blocks B_1 < ... < B_m and each
444: 36:    has an even number of reflections, then val(T_1 u ... u T_m) = sum val(T_i)
445: 37:    (each partial product is a rotation and rotations commute), and sizes add.
446: 38:(b) If B = r B' r' with r, r' rotations (values x, x') at the two ends of B and T'
447: 39:    is a selection inside B' with an even number of reflections, then
448: 40:    val({r} u T' u {r'}) = x + val(T') + x' (r' lies in a +-zone because T' has an
449: 41:    even number of reflections).
450: 42:
451: 43:## Units and their elements
452: 44:
453: 45:A *unit* is a block of consecutive positions together with an element
454: 46:theta in A given as an explicit formal sum over selections T inside the block,
455: 47:each with an even number of reflections and even size, written
456: 48:theta = sum_T c_T [phi(T)], with the empty selection having coefficient 1.
457: 49:Its *depth* is any d with theta in J^d.
458: 50:
459: 51:* single letter: theta = [0] (empty selection only), depth 0.
460: 52:* "ff" (two consecutive reflections sigma_y sigma_y'):
461: 53:  theta = [0] - [(y - y', 1)]  (selections: empty; both), depth 1.
462: 54:* "rr" (two consecutive rotations): theta = [0] - [(x + x', 1)], depth 1.
463: 55:* **Nest** N = r B' r' where B' is a concatenation of units u_1..u_m (in order)
464: 56:  with elements theta_1..theta_m:
465: 57:  theta_N := theta_1 ... theta_m ([0] - [(x + x', 1)]).
466: 58:  Expanding, every term is c [phi(T)] for the selection T = (union of the chosen
467: 59:  selections of the u_i) possibly together with {r, r'}: by Fact A(a),(b) the
468: 60:  group element is exactly phi(T) (even number of reflections, even size), and
469: 61:  the empty selection has coefficient 1.  depth(N) >= sum depth(u_i) + 1 since
470: 62:  [0] - [g] lies in J.
471: 63:
472: 64:## Lemma G (global step)
473: 65:
474: 66:Cut the cyclic word to a linear word W' and partition W' into top-level units
475: 67:u_1 < ... < u_K with elements theta_k in J^{d_k}.  If sum d_k >= 2p-1 then w has
476: 68:an identity 2p-subword.
477: 69:
478: 70:Proof.  Theta := theta_1 ... theta_K lies in J^{2p-1} = 0 (Fact L).  Expanding the
479: 71:product term by term (no collection), each term corresponds to a selection T of
480: 72:W' (union of the chosen selections), with coefficient the product of the chosen
481: 73:coefficients and group element sum phi = phi(T) by Fact A(a).  The coefficient of
482: 74:[0] in Theta is therefore 1 (the all-empty choice) plus the sum over nonempty
483: 75:choices with phi(T) = 0.  As Theta = 0, some nonempty T has phi(T) = 0:
484: 76:val(T) = 0 and |T|/2 = 0 mod p.  T is nonempty of even size at most 4p-2 (it misses
485: 77:at least the positions not in W'; in any case |W'| <= 4p-1 and |T| is even), so
486: 78:1 <= |T|/2 <= 2p-1, whence |T| = 2p; T has an even number of reflections and
487: 79:product rho_0 = 1.  Undo the cyclic cut.  []
488: 80:
489: 81:## Proof of Theorem T0
490: 82:
491: 83:Case 1: exactly one F-run F* has odd length.  Then F is odd and R = 4p-1-F is
492: 84:even.  Remove the first letter of F* as a single-letter unit and cut the cyclic
493: 85:word there; the linear word W' (length 4p-2) begins with the remaining l*-1 (even)
494: 86:letters of F* and every F-run of W' has even length.
495: 87:Case 2: every F-run has even length.  Then F is even and R is odd.  Remove any
496: 88:rotation as a single-letter unit and cut there.  F-runs of w adjacent to it
497: 89:appear at the two ends of W' (or merge cyclically, but in W' each piece is a
498: 90:whole even run), so every F-run of W' has even length and W' has an even number
499: 91:of rotations.
500: 92:
501: 93:In both cases tile W': split every F-run of W' into consecutive pairs ("ff"
502: 94:units).  List the rotations of W' in order r_1, ..., r_{2m}; for each i form the
503: 95:nest N_i = r_{2i-1} [ff units strictly between r_{2i-1} and r_{2i}] r_{2i}
504: 96:(between two consecutive rotations there is at most one F-run, which is whole and
505: 97:even).  The remaining ff units are top-level.  Every letter of W' lies in exactly
506: 98:one top-level unit.  Total depth = (#ff units) + (#rotation pairs)
507: 99:= F'/2 + R'/2 = (4p-2)/2 = 2p-1, where F', R' count letters of W'.  Lemma G
508: 100:applies.  []
509: 101:
510: 102:## Lemma TRI (sufficient fullness criterion, for later use)
511: 103:
512: 104:Let a unit have option set S (its selections' phi-values, i.e. points of H) and
513: 105:let D < p.  If for every h = 1..D the "level" {v : (v,h) in S} has at least
514: 106:D+1-h elements, then the unit admits theta in J^D supported on S with
515: 107:coefficient 1 at the empty selection ("depth D").
516: 108:Proof.  Use the point-indexed ansatz theta = [0] + sum_{s in S, s != 0} c_s [s]
517: 109:(one selection representative per nonzero point; for units shorter than 2p the
518: 110:empty selection is the only one with phi = 0).  theta lies in J^D iff
519: 111:sum_s c_s P(s) = 0 for every polynomial P of total degree < D (binomial basis
520: 112:C(v,i)C(h,j) of such polynomials), so such c exists iff every polynomial g of
521: 113:degree <= D-1 vanishing on S\{0} vanishes at 0 (linear-algebra duality; only the
522: 114:sufficient direction is used below).  Under the level hypothesis, g(.,1) has
523: 115:degree <= D-1 and >= D zeros, so (h-1) | g; inductively (h-1)...(h-D) | g,
524: 116:impossible unless g = 0.  []
525: 117:[Review fix, Codex 2026-10-17 review/codex-T0-R: duality stated for the
526: 118:point-indexed ansatz.]
527: 119:
528: 120:## Computer sanity checks [C] (not part of the proof)
529: 121:
530: 122:* code/tiling.py exact depth routine: 900 random nests r[U]r (U = up to two rr/ff
531: 123:  units, p in {5,7,11}) all have depth = len/2.
532: 124:* 800 random words of length 4p-1 (p in {5,7}) with at most one odd F-run: every
533: 125:  one has an identity 2p-subword by exact DP (code/coverage_all.has_solution).

541: 4:
542: 5:## Exact strongest statement
543: 6:
544: 7:For every prime p>=5, every word of length 4p-1 in D_(2p) whose rotation letters lie in one set {rho_x,rho_-x}, x nonzero, and whose **maximal cyclic reflection runs are each constant**, contains 2p letters in increasing original index order with ordered product identity. Different reflection runs may have different values. This is RGR-LONG(p), not the statement for arbitrary words.
545: 8:
546: 9:The accepted intermediate statements are precisely v4 §1.5 and Theorems A–C / Corollaries B', D, E:
547: 10:- NMH(p), including both R>=2p-2 and the previously accepted R<=2p-3 branch.
548: 11:- MIX_DEF_HIGH(p), MIX_DEF(p), and solvability for every RGR word of length4p-2 with F,R>=1, normalized A>=B and 1<=B<kappa, with arbitrary reflection parities.
549: 12:- RUNS(p): every **inherited** avoider in RGR_(4p-2)(p), F,R>=1, has k<=p.
550: 13:- Such inherited avoiders normalize to B=kappa=0; combining RUNS with the previously accepted PAR gives INV_Res(p): all 2k runs are odd and k<=p.
551: 14:- DR-Res then gives RGR-LONG(p).
552: 15:
553: 16:“Inherited” retains all five conditions in v4 §1.3: R<=3p-2; F+sum floor(r_i/2)<=3p-2; R+H<=3p-2; H<=p-1; and a_i,b_i<=p-1. The proof shows some are automatic, but this verdict does not erase them from the theorem statement.
554: 17:
555: 18:**Not established:** INV*(p), either inverse-normalization half; full ordered Olson for D_(2p); arbitrary finite groups; p=3; non-inherited INV(p) or MIX_RUNS(p); formal verification; novelty or priority. A full dihedral conclusion still needs to force an arbitrary avoider into the RGR class.

570: 33:
571: 34:### Parent derivation of the assembly and critical interfaces
572: 35:
573: 36:**HW/H.** Fix a sign pattern P with2m reflection-boundary changes and residual pair capacity h. Select C reflections from the even interval[2m,2m+2h], then n=2p-C rotations. With E+ and E- available positive/negative contributions, x=(a_+-a_-)/2=a_+-n/2 is realizable exactly when the four integer bounds on a_+ and C intersect. Eliminating C gives
574: 37:
575: 38:    -min(p-m, E-+m+h-p, floor(E-/2)) <= x
576: 39:      <= min(p-m, E++m+h-p, floor(E+/2)).
577: 40:
578: 41:Parity is already even in C and n; no extra parity obstruction is lost. GR supplies the signed reflection sum, and division by2 is legitimate modulo odd p. For P empty/full, endpoints can reach±p only when A or B>=2p. On an avoider A,B<=2p-1, so zero avoidance is exactly min(A,B)<=kappa. This verifies the scope of the old correction; it does not remove that scope.
579: 42:
580: 43:**Fact Z.** Normalize A>=B. If both A,B>=kappa+1, zero lies in the empty-pattern interval, contradicting avoidance; hence0<=B<=kappa. Since R=2p-o_F+2kappa=A+B<=2p-1+B, obtain o_F>=2kappa-B+1>=kappa+1. For B<kappa this improves to o_F>=kappa+2. In particular OR can be used, and its reduced all-odd word satisfies the low-branch kappa bound automatically.
581: 44:
582: 45:**NMH and MIX_DEF_HIGH.** The integer R split is exact: R>=2p-2 uses FW+ (with FWH and BR/R2); R<=2p-3 uses GC with the **entire** NMH setting. OR preserves R,F,A,B,H,kappa and avoidance; M0 lowers(A,B,R,kappa) by(1,1,2,1), raises H by1, preserves kappa-B and A-B, and stops with pure/(1,1) rotation runs. If the reduced B is positive, all NMH hypotheses hold. If it is zero, R=A<=2p-1, so C0-B' or A1 applies; the purported R>=2p exit is vacuous. No inherited condition is transported through M0.
583: 46:
584: 47:**RUNS coverage.** Under0<=B<=kappa there are only B=0<kappa, B=kappa=0, B=kappa>=1, and0<B<kappa; splitting the last two by integer o_F<=p / >=p+1 yields exactly the six branches. C1 uses OR and the all-positive lemmas. C3 is KB on the original word and retains D0 there; C4 is KBH; C5 uses OR and accepted MIX_low with kappa<=o_F-2; C6 is MIX_DEF_HIGH. C2 assumes k>=p+1 and B=kappa=0: R=2p-o_F>=k implies1<=o_F<=p-1 and R>=p+1>=6. Thus there is an even reflection run, and K0's two uses of k<=p are replaced by R>=5 and o_F<=p. Its parity step needs only e_R≡e_F mod2, automatic here. K0's old use of(P) to infer an even reflection run is unnecessary. D0 in C3 is the only inherited condition not automatic for avoiders.
585: 48:
586: 49:**DR-Res.** For a length4p-1 RGR avoider, the classical EGZ/gap/Doubling/Davenport arguments establish all inherited conditions. Deleting only within a run of length>=2 changes no run count and cannot create a zero-product subword; each such deletion is inherited and length4p-2. RUNS gives k<=p, then PAR makes every run in each deletion odd. Two different long runs would force opposite parities for an unchanged run across the two deletions, impossible. Thus just one run is long, of length4p-2k>=2p. A reflection run supplies2p identical reflections; a rotation run contains p equal rotations and the reviewed Doubling lemma applies. Both contradict avoidance. Cyclic rotations change products by conjugation, so identity selections return to increasing original index order.
587: 50:
588: 51:## Errata adopted without rewriting frozen files
589: 52:
590: 53:The seven Claude/two Codex assembly findings are already repaired in v4; the foundation findings and delta nits are adopted here:
591: 54:1. **m1 EGZ:** select p zero-sum rotations from R>=3p-1 using EGZ(n=p), then another p from the remaining>=2p-1. Their union has2p rotations and product identity. Do not cite EGZ(n=2p) at this shorter length.
592: 55:2. **m2 marked formula:** call it the single-mark variant; the product is rho_V in the chosen cyclic frame and a conjugate in the original order. The invariant assertion is “identity iff V=0.”
593: 56:3. **m3 Doubling:** choose a P-gap (for example t0) as the fixed gap; do not fix an M-gap when using the displayed union-of-colourings argument.
594: 57:4. **m4 Davenport-alpha:** state N<=4p-1 and F>=2; begin the frame at a gap outside I. A zero-sum item subset then has size2p, not an uncontrolled multiple of2p. All load-bearing applications have N=4p-1.
595: 58:5. **m5 OR:** use necessity of HW/H on the original and sufficiency on the reduced word. Empty-pattern conditions refer to the identical interval I(empty); the min(A,B)<=kappa shorthand needs A,B<=2p-1, true for avoiders.
596: 59:6. **m6 reflection pairs:** rotate the wrapping cyclic reflection run before pairing, or conjugate the selection back; do not assert every chosen pair is linearly adjacent before choosing the frame.
597: 60:7. **DC-1:** v4:122's “only Delta0>=3 and k>=7” is false as an unrestricted statement. GC also uses the full NMH setting and the identities named in GC verdict erratum5. Theorem A and B's reduced word satisfy all those hypotheses.
598: 61:8. **DC-2–DC-5:** D0 is the only **nonautomatic** inherited hypothesis; the S1 Steps3–4 Codex check is its C0-B' paragraph; the change log omits two cosmetic changes; and C2's re-justification plus the new non-inherited B=0 remark are not merely wording. No theorem statement changes.
599: 62:9. The old “not independently reviewed” theorem-P header and v4's missing-second-family status paragraphs are historical; this verdict and the foundation coverage supersede them. They are not retroactively relabelled preregistered.

692: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/olson-ordered-egz/README.md
693: SHA256 15ad2d32ee0e38069c06b7f12ac06e9e3bb4556e16658dc3d78fbf5cba0036e7
694: LF 11-42
695: 11:## 2. Problem
696: 12:
697: 13:For every finite group G, n=|G|, and every ordered sequence a_1,...,a_(2n-1), does there exist
698: 14:
699: 15:    1 <= i_1 < ... < i_n <= 2n-1,
700: 16:    a_(i_1) * ... * a_(i_n) = 1_G ?
701: 17:
702: 18:The product follows increasing original indices. An unordered multiset, an arbitrary permutation of the chosen elements, or an identity-product sequence of another length does not meet the target. One exact finite group and one avoiding word of this precise length would refute the full conjecture.
703: 19:
704: 20:Faithful Lean proposition, using actual group multiplication and ordered List.Sublist:
705: 21:
706: 22:```lean
707: 23:∀ (G : Type) [Group G] [Fintype G] (xs : List G),
708: 24:  xs.length = 2 * Fintype.card G - 1 →
709: 25:  ∃ ys : List G, ys.Sublist xs ∧
710: 26:    ys.length = Fintype.card G ∧ ys.prod = 1
711: 27:```
712: 28:
713: 29:## 3. Status and novelty gate
714: 30:
715: 31:- The [2006 Mohar listing](https://www.sfu.ca/~mohar/Problems/P0604_OlsonConjecture.html), [2007 Open Problem Garden](https://openproblemgarden.org/op/olsons_conjecture), and [2015 Halasz primary problem-session abstract](https://canadam.org/archives/2015f/program/abs/op/) state the exact ordered conjecture. The2015 abstract says it remains open apart from special cases.
716: 32:- The dated search on2026-10-14 found no general resolution. This is not proof of present openness or of novelty. [Search and access record](evidence/source-observations.json).
717: 33:- Original Olson1976 bibliographic metadata was recovered, but not its body: publisher routes returned403, API alternatives returned400/406 or metadata only, and a separate browser attempt stopped at a CAPTCHA. The exact original wording was therefore corroborated through primary later research/problem statements, not directly read from the1976 paper.
718: 34:- The odd-dihedral lower bound reproduced here was already claimed in Agarwal–Lou2016's primary abstract. Their full proof and construction were not exposed. **No new mathematical discovery is claimed.**
719: 35:
720: 36:## 4. State of the art
721: 37:
722: 38:The key recovered baseline is [Dimitrov2004](sources/dimitrov-2004.pdf), Theorem2, visually checked on p131: **the ordered conjecture holds for all finite p-groups**. For noncyclic p-groups of order n, the paper improves the threshold to (1+(2p-1)/p^2)n-1. Its soluble-group corollary is explicitly unordered. Thus small Q8/order-eight dihedral checks do not add new group coverage.
723: 39:
724: 40:[Zhao–Wang2026, arXiv2603.21052](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21052) defines its constant using products “in some order”; it does not settle this question. The2026 revision of [Godara–Joshi–Mazumdar](https://arxiv.org/html/2408.13558v2) separately studies ordered Davenport constants (arbitrary nonempty lengths) and unordered Gao constants (length|G|). These cannot be combined without a new argument.
725: 41:
726: 42:[References and math/ transfer audit](sources/references.md) record exact source boundaries. No covering local or remote openai/math entry was found. The finite-certificate example in `math/lean/ComparatorChallenges/SnakyCertificate` informed statement binding, not the mathematics of Olson's problem.

733: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/olson-ordered-egz/proof.md
734: SHA256 b2777c6b92b5da59b7eac83a19edcbb070a76cf7897092a8177bce0c5ed46405
735: LF 3-17
736: 3:## Exact target
737: 4:
738: 5:Let G be a finite group and n=|G|. Given every word a_1,...,a_(2n-1), select indices i_1<...<i_n such that a_(i_1)...a_(i_n)=1, without rearrangement. Equivalently, every list xs of length 2|G|-1 has a List.Sublist ys of length |G| and ordered product 1. No all-groups proof or counterexample has been established by this dossier.
739: 6:
740: 7:## 1. Sharp lower bound for every odd dihedral parameter (known result, reproduced)
741: 8:
742: 9:Let m>=1 be odd. In D_(2m)=<r,s | r^m=s^2=1, srs=r^(-1)>, consider the word
743: 10:
744: 11:    W = r repeated (2m-1) times, then s repeated (2m-1) times.
745: 12:
746: 13:Its length is 4m-2=2|G|-2, ONE TERM SHORT of the conjecture's hypothesis.
747: 14:
748: 15:An ordered 2m-term subword of W is r^k s^l with k+l=2m and 0<=k,l<=2m-1. In particular 0<k,l<2m. If its product were 1, projection to D_(2m)/<r> forces l even. Then s^l=1 and r^k=1, so m divides k. The only positive multiple of m below 2m is m itself. Thus k=m and l=m, contradicting oddness. Therefore W has no ordered |G|-term identity-product subword.
749: 16:
750: 17:The theorem `OlsonPartial.odd_dihedral_sharp_lower_bound` in `work/DihedralLowerBound.lean` proves the full all-m assertion with Mathlib's actual DihedralGroup, including its cardinality, the precise word length, and the universal List.Sublist quantifier. It is not a theorem about the 2|G|-1 bound. Its known-result label is essential: Agarwal–Lou's 2016 primary abstract already reports the odd-dihedral lower bound 4m-1 for the forcing constant. The abstract does not expose their construction or full proof, so this is a reproduced bound, not a claim that their exact proof was recovered.
